But concerning these things, it is necessary to know how the human body is distributed among the planets and signs. Know therefore that according to the tradition of the Arabs, the Sun rules over the brain, the heart, the thigh, the marrow, the right eye, and the spirit of life <term>spiritui vitae: the vital breath or pneuma believed to animate the body</term>. Mercury rules over the tongue, the mouth, and the other instruments or organs of the senses—both internal and external—as well as the hands, feet, shins, nerves, and the power of imagination <term>uirtuti phantasticae: the faculty of forming mental images and processing sensory data</term>. Saturn rules the spleen, the milt <note>original: "lieni"; often used synonymously with the spleen, but here listed as a distinct anatomical region</note>, the stomach, the bladder, the womb, the right ear, and the receptive power itself. Jupiter rules the liver and the fleshier part of the stomach, as well as the belly and the navel. For this reason, antiquity relates that an image of a navel was preserved in the temple of Jupiter Ammon <note>Jupiter Ammon was a syncretic deity of the Roman Jupiter and the Egyptian Amun; his famous oracle in the Libyan desert was visited by Alexander the Great</note>. There are furthermore those who also attribute the ribs, the pubic bone <term>pecten: literally 'the comb,' referring to the pubic region or the pelvic bone</term>, the intestines, blood, arms, the right hand, the left ear, and the natural power <note>the faculty governing basic biological functions like growth and nutrition</note> to him. But Mars is set over the blood, the veins, the kidneys, the gallbladder <note>original: "Chysti fellis," a variation of the anatomical term cystis fellea</note>, the buttocks, the back, the flow of seed, and the irascible power <term>ui irascibili: the soul's faculty associated with anger, courage, and spiritedness</term>. Again, Venus rules the kidneys, the testicles, the vulva, the womb, the generative seed, and the concupiscible power <term>ui concupiscibili: the soul's faculty of desire, attraction, and pleasure</term>; moreover, the flesh, fat, belly, pubic bone, navel, and all such things that serve the work of Venus <note>referring to sexual reproduction and physical love</note>, such as the sacrum bone, and
*spine* <note>catchword: referring to the spine or backbone</note>

...the spine and the loins; furthermore, she <note>This refers to Venus, whose description began on the previous page</note> is said to preside over the head and the mouth, by which the kiss—the pledge of love—is given. The Moon, indeed, although she claims the entire body and its individual members for herself according to the variety of the signs, nevertheless specifically assigned to her are the brain, the lungs, the marrow, the spine, the stomach, and the menses, and all excrements, as well as the left eye and the power of growth.

Hermes himself <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Egyptian sage and purported author of the Hermetic corpus</note> says there are seven openings in the head of a living creature, distributed among the seven planets: namely, the right ear to Saturn, the left to Jupiter, the right nostril to Mars, the left to Venus, the right eye to the Sun, the left to the Moon, and the mouth to Mercury.

In the same way, the individual signs of the zodiac care for their own members: thus Aries rules the head and the face, Taurus the neck, Gemini the arms and shoulders, Cancer presides over the chest, lungs, stomach, and the upper arms <note>original: "lacertis"</note>, Leo presides over the heart, stomach, liver, and back, Virgo looks after the intestines and the bottom of the stomach, Libra governs the kidneys, the upper thigh, and the buttocks, Scorpio the genitals, the vulva, and the womb, Sagittarius rules the thigh and the groin <note>original: "subinguinibus"</note>, Capricorn rules the knees, Aquarius the legs and shins, and Pisces rule the feet.

And just as the triplicities <term>triplicitates: groups of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (fire, earth, air, water)</term> of these signs correspond to one another and agree in the heavens, so they also agree in the bodily members. This is well-established by experience: for when the stomach and chest ache because of cold feet, these members correspond to the same triplicity <note>Cancer (chest/stomach) and Pisces (feet) both belong to the Water triplicity; thus, an affliction in one sign's body part can be felt in another sign's part within the same element</note>. Therefore, a remedy applied to one benefits the other; for example, when the feet are warmed, the pain ceases...

Even the activities and characters of men are distributed according to the planets: for **Saturn** rules old men, monks, and those prone to melancholy, as well as hidden treasures and things acquired through long journeys and great difficulty. **Jupiter**, however, governs the religious, prelates, kings, and leaders, as well as wealth acquired through lawful means. **Mars** rules barbers, surgeons, doctors, bailiffs <note>original: "lictores"</note>, executioners, butchers, oven-keepers, bakers, and soldiers, who are commonly called "Martial" <note>original: "Mauortij," an archaic poetic name for those belonging to Mars</note>. ### How it is to be known which natural things are subject to which stars, and which things are Solar. Chapter 23.

But to know which thing is subject to which star or zodiac sign is exceedingly difficult. Nevertheless, they are identified through their imitation of the rays, the motion, or the shape of the higher celestial bodies. Some are also known by their colors and odors, and others by the effects of their operations, which harmonize with certain stars. 

Thus, among the elements, things that are **Solar** <note>Belonging to the influence of the Sun</note> are fire and the bright flame. Among the humors <term>humors: the four primary fluids—blood, phlegm, yellow bile, and black bile—thought to control health and temperament</term>, Solar things are the purest blood and the **spirit of life**. Among tastes, it is that which is sharp, yet mixed with sweetness. 

Among metals, **gold** is Solar because of its brilliance; it also receives from the Sun the power to be a strengthener of the heart. Among stones, those are Solar which imitate the sun’s rays with golden specks, such as the **Eagle Stone** <note>original: "lapis Aetites"</note>. This stone, imitating the Sun with its golden droplets, has power against the **falling sickness** <term>falling sickness: an early term for epilepsy</term> and against poisons. 

So too is the stone called the **Eye of the Sun** <note>original: "Solis oculus"</note>, which has a shape similar to the pupil of an eye, from the center of which a ray flashes; it strengthens the brain and aids the sight. Likewise, the **Carbuncle** <note>original: "Carbunculus"; likely a ruby or deep red garnet</note>, which shines at night, has power against every airy and vaporous poison. So also the **Chrysolite** stone <note>original: "Chrysolithus"; likely peridot</note>, which possesses a thin and bright green color, within which a golden star flashes when it is held against the Sun, strength—

It possesses a marvelous power against poisons, and it makes the wearer glorious and formidable to their adversaries.

By others it is called ***Pantherus***, as it is likened to the spotted animal, the ***Panther***. It is also called ***Pantherus*** because it contains all colors; ***Aaron*** <note>Likely referring to a medieval authority on gemstones</note> calls it the ***Evanthos*** <note>original: "Euanthien"; meaning "well-flowered" or "brightly colored" in Greek</note>.

Likewise, ***Orpiment*** <note>original: "Auri pigmentum"; a golden-yellow mineral used as a pigment</note> and golden colors, and things of much light, are solar. Truly, among plants and trees, those are solar which turn toward the sun, such as the ***heliotrope***, and those which fold their leaves into themselves when the Sun recedes, but unfold them gradually when it rises: such as the ***Lotus***, which the shape of both its fruit and leaves testifies is a solar shrub.

In this way, the ***Peony***, ***Celandine***, ***Citron***, ***Ginger***, ***Gentian***, ***Dittany***, and also ***Vervain***—which is excellent for prophecy and purification, and for putting evil spirits <note>original: "cacodaemones"</note> to flight—are solar. So too is the ***Bay Laurel*** sacred to ***Phoebus*** <note>Apollo</note>, as well as ***Cedar***, the ***Palm***, the ***Ash***, and ***I-***

The Bull was consecrated to the Sun by the Egyptians at ***Heliopolis*** <note>The "City of the Sun"</note>, where they name it ***Mnevis*** <note>original: "Veritem"; Agrippa refers to the sacred bull of Heliopolis</note>; and there is the ***Apis*** bull in ***Memphis***, and in ***Hermonthis*** the Bull named ***Pathim*** <note>Likely the Buchis bull</note>. The ***Wolf*** also is sacred to ***Apollo*** and ***Latona***.

Likewise, among birds, the ***Solar*** ones are the ***Phoenix*** (the unique bird), and the ***Eagle*** (the queen of birds); also the ***Vulture***, the ***Swan***, and those birds which applaud the rising Sun with certain hymns, as it were, and seem to call upon it, such as the ***Cock*** and the ***Raven***. The ***Hawk*** also, because in Egyptian theology it is a symbol of spirit and light, is numbered among the ***Solar*** creatures by ***Porphyry*** <note>A 3rd-century Neoplatonist philosopher</note>. 

Furthermore, ***Solar*** are whatever things gather a likeness of the Sun’s works, such as ***glow-worms*** at night; also the ***Scarab*** beetle, which they call the ***Cat*** because of its shining form, and which they also call a ***Cantharus*** <note>A type of dung beetle</note>, which rolls little balls and sits upon them...

### Which things are subject to the Moon. Chapter 24.

Truly, those things that are **Lunar** among the elements are earth, and then water—both of the sea and of rivers—and all moist things: the humors of trees and animals, especially the white ones, such as the whites of eggs, fats, sweats, phlegm <note>original: "pituitae"</note>, and the superfluities <note>excess fluids or waste products</note> of bodies. Among tastes, the salty and the insipid. Among metals, truly, silver. 

Among stones, her own are **Crystal**, silver **Marcasite** <note>A metallic mineral often confused with pyrite in historical texts</note>, and all white and green stones. Likewise the **Selenite** stone <note>original: "lapis Selenites"</note>, that is, the **Moon-stone**, which is translucent white with a honey-like glow; it imitates the lunar motion, having the shape of the moon within itself, and showing it change every day according to the number of its waxing or waning. Likewise **pearls**, which are generated from drops of water in shells; and similarly **Crystal** and **Beryl**. 

Among plants and trees, the **Lunar** ones are the **Selenotrope** <note>original: "Selenotropia"</note>, which turns toward the moon just as the **Heliotrope** turns toward the Sun; and the **Palm tree**, which at every rising of the Moon, produces a single [branch]... <note>original catchword: "ramum" (branch)</note>

shooting forth a branch: Hyssop is also a species of rosemary, the smallest tree and the largest of herbs, participating in the nature of both. The **Agnus Castus** <note>original: "Agnus castus"; also known as the Chaste-tree, historically used to preserve virginity and linked to the Moon's cold, damp nature</note> is also lunar, as is the **Olive**. Similarly, there is the herb **Chmostates** <note>original: "Chmo-states"; likely a reference to a legendary lunar plant, sometimes identified as a variety of "lunaria" or moonwort</note>, which grows and shrinks along with the Moon—specifically in its physical substance and the number of its leaves, not merely in its moisture and vital power. This is a trait which individual plants share in some manner, except for **Martial onions** <note>original: "caepas Martias"; onions associated with Mars</note>, which alone possess the contrary power of shrinking when the Moon waxes and growing when she wanes. Just as among birds, the **Oryx** <note>original: "Orygis"; while usually a desert mammal, in this tradition it is described as a Saturnian bird hostile to the luminaries</note>, a bird of Saturn, is most hostile to both the Moon and the Sun alike. 

**Lunar animals** are those that take pleasure in human company, and those that equally excel in various natural loves and hatreds, such as **dogs** of every kind. The **Chameleon** is also lunar, which, according to the variety of the color of an object, always puts on a similar hue, just as the Moon varies her nature according to the variety of the zodiacal sign in which she is found. Likewise lunar are **Sows**, **Does**, **She-goats**, and whatever animals observe and imitate the lunar movement, such as the **Cynocephalus** <note>The dog-headed baboon, famously associated with the Egyptian god Thoth and the Moon</note> and the **Panther**. For it is said that the panther has a spot on its shoulder like the Moon, growing into circles and curving into horns in the same manner. **Cats** are also lunar, whose eyes become larger or smaller according to the phases of the Moon. Other things of this kind are lunar as well, such as **menstrual blood**, with which many wonderful and monstrous things are performed by Magicians. The **Hyena** is also included, which changes its sex and is subject to various sorceries, and...

The **sparrow** is also sacred to Venus, which by command of the law was given for the purification of leprosy, that "evil of Mars" <note>In ancient medicine, leprosy was considered a "hot" disease ruled by Mars; the sparrow of Venus was the cooling remedy</note> against which nothing resists so effectively. The Egyptians also call the **Eagle** "Venus" because it is so prone to love; for even if mounted thirteen times in a day, if the male calls, it runs to him again. Among fish, those of Venus are the lustful **Cranefish**, the most lecherous **Parrot-fish** <note>original: "Scari"</note>, the **Sea-thrushes** <note>original: "Merulæ"</note> on account of their love for their offspring, the **Sea-bream** <note>original: "Cancharus"</note> which fights for its mate, and the **Tithymallus** <note>A plant or sea-growth associated with Euphorbia</note> on account of the fragrance and sweetness of its scent.

## OF OCCULT PHILOSOPHY

**Mercurial things** among the elements are water, although it moves all things distinctly; similarly it regulates the humors, especially those that are mixed, but it also governs the **animal spirit** <note>In Renaissance physiology, the "animal spirit" was a refined substance that flowed through the nerves to facilitate sensation and movement</note>, and various, foreign, and mixed flavors. Among metals, belonging to Mercury are **quicksilver** <note>original: "argentum uiuum"</note>, **tin**, and **silver marcasite**. Among stones are the **Emerald**, **Agate**, **Porphyry**, **Topaz**, and those of various colors, or those which have various figures implanted by nature, or those made by art, such as **glass**, and those that mix saffron-yellow with green.

Among plants and trees are the **Hazel**, **Cinquefoil** <note>original: "Pentaphyllon"</note>, **Mercury** <note>the herb *Mercurialis*</note>, **Fumitory** <note>original: "fumus terræ"</note>, **Burnet** <note>original: "pimpinella"</note>, **Marjoram**, **Parsley** <note>original: "Petrosilinũ"</note>, and those with shorter and smaller leaves, composed of mixed natures and various colors. 

Animals that are sagacious, ingenious, hardworking, of a versatile nature, fast-running, and which easily become accustomed to humans belong to Mercury, such as **Dogs**, **Monkeys**, **Mice**, **Weasels**, the **Deer**, and the **Mule**; also hermaphroditic animals and those that change their sex in turn, such as the **Hare**, the **Hyena**, and the like. 

Among birds are those that are by nature ingenious, songful, and musical, and versatile, such as **Goldfinches**, **Fig-eaters** <note>original: "Ficedulæ"</note>, the **Blackbird**, the **Thrush**, the **Lark**, the **Nightingale**, the **Calandra Lark**, the **Parrot**, the **Magpie**, the **Ibis**, the **Purple Swamphen** <note>original: "Porphyrio"</note>, and the **Rhinoceros Beetle** <note>original: "Scarabeus unicornus"; ancient and Renaissance naturalists often categorized flying insects alongside birds</note>. 

Among fish, however, is the **Trochus** <note>A sea creature described by ancient naturalists like Pliny as being able to fertilize itself</note>, which enters into itself, wherefore the males call to the female. Likewise the fraudulent **Octopus** <note>original: "Polypus"</note>, versatile in color, and the **Pasmaca** <note>A type of fish noted in classical texts for its cleverness in avoiding nets</note> on account of its cleverness; similarly also the **Mullet** <note>original: "mugil"</note>, which shakes the food off the hook with its tail.

->BOOK I.<-

### That the entire sublunary world <note>The "sublunary world" refers to the Earth and its atmosphere, the region below the Moon's sphere which was believed to be subject to change and decay, unlike the eternal heavens.</note>, and those things which are in it, are distributed among the planets. Chapter 30.

Furthermore, whatever is found in the whole world is influenced and receives its power according to the lordships of the planets. Thus, in fire, the life-giving light belongs to the **Sun**, and heat to **Mars**; in earth, the varied surface belongs to the **Moon**, **Mercury**, and the **starry heavens**, but the entire mass belongs to **Saturn**. But in the middle elements <note>The "middle elements" are Air and Water, which sit between the extremes of Fire (above) and Earth (below).</note>, where moisture rules, the airy part is subject to **Jupiter**, the watery part to the **Moon**, and the mixed part obeys **Mercury** and **Venus**.

By a similar reasoning, in the nature of things, the **active causes** <note>In Aristotelian and Renaissance philosophy, these are the forces that initiate action or define the form of a thing, as opposed to passive matter.</note> follow the **Sun**, while **matter** follows the **Moon**. The fecundity of active causes belongs to **Jupiter**, and the fecundity of matter to **Venus**. Swift dispatch toward an effect belongs to **Mars** and **Mercury**: the former because of his intensity, the latter because of his dexterity and multifaceted power. However, the lasting continuation of all things is dedicated to **Saturn**.

But also among the number of vegetables <note>In this historical context, "vegetables" refers to the entire plant kingdom, including trees and herbs.</note>, everything that bears fruit is from **Jupiter**; and everything that bears flowers is from **Venus**; and every seed and bark is from **Mercury**; while every root is from **Saturn**, and all wood from **Mars**, and the leaves from the **Moon**. Hence, all things that bear fruit and do not flower belong to **Saturn** and **Jupiter**; those that flower and produce seeds, but do not produce fruit, belong to **Venus** and **Mercury**; and those that are produced spontaneously without seed belong to the **Moon** and **Saturn**. All beauty is from **Venus**, and strength is from **Mars**, and every...

BOOK I.

With **Taurus**, [Mars] has the Cyclades, the coastal regions of Asia Minor, Cyprus, Parthia, Media, and Persia. When he rules with **Libra**, however, he governs the Bactrians, the Caspians, Syria, the Thebaid <note>A region in ancient Upper Egypt, named after the city of Thebes.</note>, the Oases, and the Troglodytes <note>Literally "cave-dwellers"; a term used by ancient geographers for various tribes, particularly those along the Red Sea coast.</note>. **Mercury** with **Gemini** holds Hyrcania, Armenia, Mantiana, Cyrenaica, Marmarica, and Lower Egypt. Truly with **Virgo**, he holds Greece, Achaia, Crete, Babylon, Mesopotamia, Assyria, and Elam—from which the Elamites in Scripture are named. Following the **Moon** with **Cancer** are Bithynia, Phrygia, Colchis, Numidia, Africa, Carthage, and all of Carchedonia <note>original: "Carchedonia." This is the Greek name for the territory of Carthage.</note>. These things we have gathered according to the opinion of Ptolemy <note>Claudius Ptolemy (c. 100–170 AD), whose work *Tetrabiblos* established the foundations of Western astrological geography.</note>, to which many other things could be added according to the writings of other astrologers. But whoever knows how to compare these divisions of provinces upon this celestial distribution with the ministries of the governing Intelligences <note>Term: "Intelligences." In Renaissance magic, these are the spiritual or angelic beings that direct the influence of the planets.</note>, the blessings of the Israelite tribes, the lots of the Apostles <note>Refers to the tradition of assigning the twelve tribes of Israel and the twelve Apostles to the twelve signs of the Zodiac.</note>, and the symbolic seals of the Holy Scriptures, will be able to draw out great, even prophetic, oracles of future events for each region.

### What Things are Subject to the Signs, Fixed Stars, and Their Images. Chapter 32.

A similar reasoning is to be held in all things concerning the figures of the fixed stars: thus they maintain that the earthly ram is subject to the celestial **Aries**; and the earthly bull and ox to the celestial **Taurus**; and similarly crabs to **Cancer**;

It begins a new chapter on the sympathetic relationship between earthly animals and the constellations that share their names.</summary>

<header>OF OCCULT PHILOSOPHY</header>

Lions belong to **Leo**, virgins to **Virgo**, and **Scorpio** presides over scorpions; likewise, **Capricorn** rules over goats <note>original: "Capricornis"</note>, **Sagittarius** over horses, and the fish are subject to the sign of **Pisces**. In the same way, the celestial Bear <note>The constellations Ursa Major and Ursa Minor.</note> presides over bears, the Hydra over serpents, and dogs are subject to the Dog-star <note>Sirius, in the constellation Canis Major.</note>; thus it is for all individual things. 

Apuleius <note>Pseudo-Apuleius, the traditional author of a highly influential 4th-century herbal manual.</note> himself distributed specific and particular herbs to the signs and the planets. To **Aries**, he assigned sage <note>original: "elelisphacon"</note>; to **Taurus**, upright vervain; to **Gemini**, prostrate vervain; to **Cancer**, comfrey; to **Leo**, cyclamen; to **Virgo**, calamint; to **Libra**, scorpion-tail plant <note>original: "Scorpiurum"</note>; to **Scorpio**, mugwort; to **Sagittarius**, pimpernel; to **Capricorn**, dock; to **Aquarius**, dragonwort; and to **Pisces**, birthwort. 

Regarding the planets: to **Saturn**, he assigned houseleek <note>original: "sempervivum," literally "ever-living."</note>; to **Jupiter**, agrimony <note>original: "Eupatorium"</note>; to **Mars**, sulfurwort <note>original: "peucedanum"</note>; to the **Sun**, heliotrope; to **Venus**, all-heal or maidenhair <note>original: "callitrichum"</note>; to **Mercury**, mullein <note>original: "flomum"</note>; and to the **Moon**, peony <note>original: "Aglaophotim," a legendary plant described as shining in the dark.</note>. 

However, Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary father of the Hermetic arts.</note>—followed by Albertus <note>Albertus Magnus (c. 1200–1280), the medieval philosopher and scientist.</note>—gives these assignments for the planets: to **Saturn**, asphodel; to **Jupiter**, henbane; to **Mars**, plantain; to the **Sun**, knotgrass <note>original: "polygoniam"</note>; to **Venus**, vervain; to **Mercury**, cinquefoil <note>original: "pentaphyllon"</note>; and to the **Moon**, dog-tongue <note>original: "chynostacen," likely a variation of cynoglossa.</note>. 

Furthermore, we know through experience that asparagus is subject to **Aries**, and basil <note>original: "Basilicon"</note> to **Scorpio**. For if the shavings of a ram's horn are sown, asparagus grows; and basil, if crushed between two stones, generates scorpions. 

In addition, from the more prominent stars—according to the teaching of Hermes and Thabit <note>Thābit ibn Qurra (826–901), a Sabian astronomer and mathematician whose works were central to medieval magic.</note>—I will list a few here. The first is called the **Head of Algol** <note>The "Demon Star" in the constellation Perseus, traditionally associated with great power and danger.</note>; among stones, it presides over the diamond, and among plants, it rules over black hellebore and mugwort.

Eleventh is called *Alchanech* <note>The star Arcturus.</note>, which presides over *Jasper* among stones;

Instead, many things receive influence from several stars, and not so much separately as in combination. Thus, among stones, **Chalcedony** is subject to **Saturn** and **Mercury**, along with the **Tail of the Scorpion** and the **Tail of Capricorn**. **Sapphire**, however, is subject to **Jupiter** and **Saturn**, and the star **Alhagoth** <note>The star Capella in the constellation Auriga.</note>. **Tutty** <note>original: "Tuthia," an impure form of zinc oxide collected from furnace flues, used in alchemy and medicine.</note> belongs to **Jupiter** and the **Luminaries** <note>The Sun and the Moon.</note>. In a similar way among vegetables, the herb **Serpentaria** <note>Likely Dragonwort or Snakeweed, often used in folklore against snake bites.</note> is subject to **Saturn** and the celestial **Serpent-bearer** <note>The constellation Ophiuchus.</note>. **Mastic** <note>A resin obtained from the mastic tree.</note> and **Mint** belong to **Jupiter** and the **Sun**, but **Mastic** also belongs to the **Heart of the Lion** <note>The star Regulus; the word is completed by the catchword "nis" on the following page.</note>

### *On the seals and characters of natural things. Chapter 33.*

All stars have their own specific natures, properties, and conditions. Through their rays, they produce their own seals <note>original: "signacula"</note> and characters <note>original: "characteres"</note> even in these lower things—in the elements, in stones, in plants, in animals, and in their various parts. Hence, every single thing, by way of a harmonious arrangement <note>original: "dispositione harmoniaca"</note> and by the star that shines upon it, receives a special seal or character impressed upon itself. This mark is a sign of that star or harmony and contains within itself a specific virtue <note>original: "uirtutem"; meaning an inherent occult power or efficacy</note> that differs from others, whether by the genus, the species, or the numerical nature of the underlying matter. Therefore, every thing has its own character impressed upon it by its star for some peculiar effect, especially by that star which holds the highest authority over it. These characters contain and retain within themselves the specific natures, virtues, and roots of their stars, and produce similar operations...

...into other things, upon which they are reflected, and they call forth and assist the influences <note>original: "influxus"</note> of their own stars, whether of the planets, or even of the fixed stars, and of the celestial images and signs—provided, of course, that they were fashioned in the proper material, at the right time, and with the required ceremonies. Considering this, the ancient sages, who labored immensely to investigate the hidden conditions of things, noted down the images, figures, signs <note>original: "signacula"</note>, seals <note>original: "sigilla"</note>, and characters <note>original: "characteres"</note> of the stars.

Nature herself depicted these through the rays of the stars in these lower realms: some in stones, others in plants—within the joints and knots of branches—and others in the various limbs of animals. For the **Laurel**, the **Lotus** <note>original: "Loton"</note>, and the **Sunflower** <note>original: "Heliostrophium," referring to plants like the heliotrope that turn toward the sun</note>, being solar plants, reveal the characters of the **Sun** in their roots and cut knots. The same is true in the bones and shoulder blades <note>original: "spatulis"</note> of animals. From this arose **Spatularia** <note>original: "Spatularia"; a form of divination known as scapulimancy, where one "reads" the markings or cracks on a shoulder blade bone</note>, a type of divination; and in stones and quarries, the characters and images of celestial bodies are not rarely found.

But since in such a vast diversity of things, knowledge cannot be easily passed down except in the few instances that can be grasped by human wisdom, we shall now leave aside those things which can be investigated in plants, stones, and other objects, or in the limbs of various animals. Instead, let us fix our focus on human nature alone. Since humanity is the most complete image of the entire universe, containing all celestial harmony within itself, there is no doubt that the signs and characters of all the stars and celestial influences are found in abundance within...

<meta>catchword: ...us (completing "reperimus," meaning "we find")</meta>

<header>BOOK I.</header>

...ledge, and these signs are indeed more effective the closer they remain to their celestial nature. But just as the number of stars is known to God alone, so too are their effects and signs <note>original: "signacula"</note> regarding these lower realms; for this reason, no human intellect can ascend to the point of knowing them all.

Consequently, very few things have become known, which the ancient philosophers and Chiromancers <term>Chiromancers: practitioners of chiromancy or palmistry, the art of reading the lines and shapes of the hand to determine character or fortune</term> attained partly through reason and partly through experience; many things still lie hidden and shut away in the treasuries of nature. Therefore, we will note here a few signs, or characters <note>original: "characteres"</note>, of the planets which the Chiromancers have recognized in the hands of men.

Julian <note>likely referring to Julian the Apostate, the 4th-century Roman Emperor known for his interest in Neoplatonism and theurgy</note> calls these "sacred" or "divine letters," since, according to the word of Holy Scripture, the life of men is written through them in their hands. These signs remain always the same, consistent, and permanent among all nations and languages, wherever they may be. To these signs, both ancient and more recent Chiromancers later added many others; whoever wishes to know these should seek out their own volumes. It is enough here to have shown from where the characters of nature derive their origin, and in what things they are to be sought.

# ON OCCULT PHILOSOPHY

### How we may entice and attract the influences and virtues of celestial bodies through natural things and their virtues, Chapter 34.

Now, if you wish to receive a <term>virtue</term> <note>Virtue: In this context, it refers to an inherent power or spiritual property, not a moral quality.</note> from any part of the world or from a star, by employing those things that pertain to that star, you undergo its own proper influence—just as wood, when prepared with sulfur, pitch, and oil, is ready to catch a flame. Nevertheless, when you properly apply many things to a specific species or individual—things which are scattered but conform to the same <term>Idea</term> <note>Idea: In the Platonic sense, the divine "blueprint" or archetype of a thing existing in the higher realms.</note> and star—a singular gift is soon infused from that Idea through the reasoning of the <term>World Soul</term> <note>original: "anima mundi"; the vital spirit believed by Renaissance philosophers to connect all living things and the cosmos.</note>, by means of this material which has been so opportunely prepared.

I say "opportunely prepared," specifically under a harmony similar to the harmony that first infused the virtue into the matter. For although things possess certain virtues of the kind we have described, those virtues are so deeply hidden that a work is rarely completed by them through such a power; but just as crushing excites the latent pungency in a mustard seed, and just as the heat of fire brings into view letters written with onion juice or milk that were previously hidden, and just as letters inscribed on a stone with goat's fat—though entirely invisible—emerge and stand out as if they were carved when the stone is submerged in vinegar, and just as the touch of a broom or a <term>strawberry tree</term> <note>original: "arbuti"</note> rouses a dormant madness, so...

<meta>catchword: ipsa (itself)</meta>

<summary>This page explains the philosophical mechanism of "sympathetic magic," where natural materials acts as a conductor for celestial influences when prepared according to cosmic harmonies. It uses several natural analogies, such as invisible ink and mustard seeds, to explain how "occult virtues" remain latent until properly activated.</summary>
<keywords>celestial influence, World Soul, anima mundi, occult virtues, natural magic, planetary harmony, Agrippa</keywords>

HEADER: BOOK I.
<sig>F 4</sig>
<meta>catchword: virtues</meta>

...the celestial harmony itself <note>original: "harmonia coelestis"</note> brings forth, excites, strengthens, and makes manifest the <term>virtue</term> <note>Virtue: In this context, it refers to an inherent spiritual power or property, not a moral quality.</note> hidden in matter. It leads it, so to speak, from <term>potentiality</term> into <term>actuality</term> <note>Potentiality to Actuality: A concept from Aristotelian philosophy where a latent possibility (potential) becomes a realized, active fact (act).</note> when things are rightly exposed to that harmony under a favorable celestial opportunity.

For example, if you wish to attract a virtue from the Sun, seek those things which are "solar" among vegetables, plants, metals, stones, and animals. These are to be applied and taken, especially those which are higher in the solar hierarchy, for these contribute more. In this way, you will draw a singular gift from the Sun through the Sun's rays—received at the opportune moment—and through the <term>spirit of the world</term> <note>original: "spiritum mundi"; the subtle substance believed to link the physical world to the celestial realms.</note>.

### *On the mixtures of natural things with one another, and their utility*
### Chapter 35.

We have discovered that in the lower nature <note>The "lower nature" refers to the earthly realm, as opposed to the "higher" celestial or divine realms.</note>, all the gifts of the higher [celestial] bodies are not found gathered within a single object. Instead, they are scattered among us across many species. For instance, there are many "solar" things, yet no single one of them contains all the virtues of the Sun; rather, one thing possesses some properties from the Sun, while another possesses others.

Therefore, it is sometimes necessary in our operations to create <term>mixtures</term> <note>original: "mixtiones"</note>. If a hundred or a thousand virtues of the Sun are scattered across as many plants, animals, and the like, we can fuse these together and reduce them into a single form, in which the said [virtues...]

Every mixture made of several parts is most perfect when it is so bonded from its components that it becomes a single thing, consistent throughout, and not easily broken apart. We see this sometimes in stones and diverse bodies that are joined and united by a certain natural force so that they seem entirely one. Examples include two trees joined by grafting, or oysters fused with stones by a hidden force of nature. Even animals have been seen turned into stone, so united with the substance of the rock that they seem to form a single, uniform body. For daily something natural is drawn through art, and daily something divine is drawn through nature; looking upon this, the Egyptians called Nature a magician, that is, the magical power itself, in the attraction of like by like and compatible things by compatible things. 

Now, this kind of attraction, through the mutual agreement of things toward one another—of the higher with the lower—the Greeks called **sympathy** <note>original: "συμπάθειαν" (sympatheian); the principle that parts of the universe are interconnected and react to one another across distances.</note>. Thus earth agrees with water in coldness; water with air in moisture; air with fire in heat; fire agrees with the heavens in matter. Nor is fire mixed with water except through air; nor air with earth except through water. In the same way, the soul is not joined to the body except through the spirit; nor the intellect to the spirit except through the soul. Thus we see that when nature has shaped the human body of the fetus, by this very preparation it immediately draws down a spirit from the universe; this spirit is the tinder in the spirit and body for attaining intelligence and a mind from the divine:

These are joined with God through immortality; divinity is joined to the mind, the mind to the intellect, this to the intention, that to the image, the image to sensation, that to the senses, and these finally to material things.

### Chapter 38. How we may receive from above not only celestial and vital gifts, but even certain intellectual and divine ones.

The **Magi** <note>original: "Magi"; here referring to the ancient practitioners of "natural magic" or wisdom, who sought to understand the hidden laws of the universe.</note> teach that through lower things that conform to higher things, celestial gifts can be drawn down by the opportune influences of the heavens. In this way, through these celestial things, celestial **daemons** <note>original: "dæmones"; in the Neoplatonic tradition used here, these are not "demons" in the modern sense, but intermediary spirits or guiding intelligences that serve the stars.</note>, insofar as they are followers of the stars, may be won over to us and introduced into our lives. 

Therefore, when certain materials possessing the natural power of divinity—that is, those which are naturally in harmony with the higher realms—are properly collected (partly through physical and partly through astronomical means) and opportunely combined from all sides, then gifts can be received from above. These include not only celestial or vital gifts, but even intellectual, certain "demonic" <note>pertaining to the intermediary spirits mentioned above</note>, and divine ones. This is confirmed by **Iamblichus**, **Proclus**, and **Synesius** <note>Three major Neoplatonic philosophers who wrote extensively on "theurgy," or the art of divine works and ritual.</note>, along with the entire school of the **Platonists**. 

For such is the harmony of the world that even super-celestial things are drawn by celestial ones, and supernatural things conspire with and are drawn by natural ones. This is because a single **craftsman-like power** <note>original: "virtus opifex"; a reference to the Demiurge or the creative force of God that built the universe.</note> and a participation in the **Forms** <note>original: "specierum"; the ideal, spiritual blueprints of all created things.</note> are diffused through all things. Indeed, just as this craftsman-like power produces manifest things from hidden reasons...

For instance, it is said that a stone born in the pupil of the **Hyena's** eye, if held under the tongue, provides the power of divination. In the same way, they say that by the **Ananchitis** <note>A legendary stone used in antiquity for hydromancy and summoning spirits.</note> the images of the Gods are called forth, and by the **Synochitis** <note>A stone believed to hold the ghosts of the dead when summoned.</note> the shadows of the underworld, once summoned, are held fast. He adds that the Magi use it when they wish to summon the Gods, and that there is another herb named **Theangelida** <note>Literally "divine messenger."</note>, which the Magi drink so that they may divine. 

Furthermore, there are even certain herbs by which the dead may be recalled to life. To this end, the historian **Xanthus** <note>Xanthus of Lydia, a 5th-century BCE historian.</note> tells of an herb named **Bali**, by which a slain dragon cub was restored to life by its parent; by that same herb, a man named **Tillo**, whom the dragon had killed, was also resuscitated. **Juba** <note>Juba II, King of Mauretania and a noted scholar of the 1st century BCE.</note> also reports that in Arabia, a man was recalled to life by a certain herb.

### Chapter 39. That we are able to attract worldly divinities and the spirits who serve them using certain physical materials.

No one is unaware that evil spirits <note>original: "dæmones"; in Agrippa's context, these are intermediary spiritual beings, often categorized as "daemons" rather than purely modern "demons."</note> can be attracted by wicked and profane arts. **Psellus** <note>Michael Psellus (c. 1017–1078), a Byzantine philosopher and historian whose works on demonology were highly influential during the Renaissance.</note> recounts how the **Gnostic** magicians used to perform detestable and abominable obscenities. Similar things were done in ancient times during the rites of **Priapus** <note>A Greek god of fertility and protector of gardens, often represented with a permanent erection.</note> and in the service of the idol called **Peor** <note>original: "Panor"; likely referring to the biblical Baal-Peor, whose worship was associated with indecency.</note>, to whom sacrifices were made with the private parts uncovered. Nor is it unlike these—if indeed it is the truth and not a fable—what is read concerning the detestable heresy of the **Knights Templar**. Similar reports are established regarding wicked women <note>original: "maleficis mulieribus"; a reference to those accused of witchcraft.</note>; indeed, the "madness of old women" is often found to wander into such outrages. By these things and others like them, evil spirits <meta>are attracted...</meta>

...spirits <note>The text begins with the second half of the word "dæmones" (spirits/demons) from the previous page.</note> are attracted and conspire together, just as the malignant spirit says concerning **Simon Magus** <note>A figure from the New Testament and apocryphal literature, often called the "father of all heresies," known for his magical feats.</note> to **John**:

No one, on the other hand, is unaware that by good works, a pure mind, mystical prayers, devout supplications, and similar things, the supercelestial angels are won over to us. Therefore, let no one doubt that in a similar way, through certain physical materials of the world, the worldly divinities can also be attracted, or at least the spirits <note>original: "dæmones"</note> who are the ministers or followers of these divinities—as **Hermes Trismegistus** <note>original: "Mercurius"; Agrippa refers here to the legendary Egyptian sage and author of the Hermetic corpus.</note> calls them, the "spirits of the air," who are not supercelestial, much less from the higher realms. 

Thus we read that the ancient priests made statues and images that predicted the future, and infused them with the **spirits of the stars** <note>original: "spiritus stellarium"; the celestial influences or intelligences associated with specific stars or constellations.</note>. These spirits were not so much trapped by certain materials as they were delighted by them; specifically, when they recognize materials that are suitable for themselves, they always and willingly dwell within them. Through these objects, they speak and perform wonders, just as spirits usually do when occupying human bodies.

---

### Chapter 40. On bindings: of what kind they are, and in what ways they are usually performed.

We have spoken about the virtues and the wonderful efficacy of natural things. It remains now to look at a matter of great marvel, and that is the **Binding** <term>ligatio: a magical operation intended to restrict, influence, or compel a person, animal, or element</term> of hu-

# OF OCCULT PHILOSOPHY #

...men <note>The text begins with the second half of the Latin word "hominum" (of men) from the previous page.</note> into love or into hate, into sicknesses and health, and such things. Likewise, the **binding** <note>original: "ligatio"; a magical technique intended to tie, hinder, or constrain an object or person.</note> of thieves and robbers, so that they cannot steal in a certain place; the **binding** of merchants, so that they cannot buy or sell in a certain place; the **binding** of an army, so that it cannot cross a certain boundary; the **binding** of ships, so that by no force of the winds—even with infinite sails stretched by the wind—are they able to leave the harbor. 

Likewise, the **binding** of a mill, so that it cannot be turned by any impulse; the **binding** of a cistern or a spring, so that water cannot be drawn from it; the **binding** of a field, so that crops cannot be produced in it; the **binding** of a certain place, so that nothing can be built there; the **binding** of fire, so that it cannot be lit in a certain place, and so that a combustible object does not burn even when placed in a very strong fire. Likewise, the **binding** of lightning and storms, so that they cannot cause harm. Likewise, the **binding** of dogs, so that they cannot bark; the **binding** of birds and wild beasts, so that they cannot fly or flee; and similar things which are scarcely believable, yet which have been frequently known through experience.

Moreover, such **bindings** are performed through <term>sorceries</term> <note>original: "ueneficia"; specifically refers to magic worked through drugs, herbs, or poisons.</note>, <term>eye-salves</term> <note>original: "collyria"; medicinal or magical washes used to alter perception or vision.</note>, ointments, potions or love-charms, through physical bindings and suspensions, through rings, through fascinations, through strong imaginations and ecstasies of the soul; through images and <term>characters</term> <note>original: "characteres"; specialized symbols or sigils believed to hold magical power.</note>, through incantations and imprecations, through lights, through sounds, through numbers, through words and names, invocations, sacrifices, adjurations, <term>exorcisms</term> <note>original: "exorcismata"; ritual commands used to constrain or expel spirits.</note>, consecrations, devotions, and through various superstitions, ritual observations, and similar methods.

BOOK I.

## On Sorceries, and Their Power
### Chapter 41.

The power of <term>sorceries</term> <note>original: "ueneficium"; a term specifically denoting magic performed through drugs, poisons, or herbal concoctions.</note> is said to be so great that they are believed to be able to subvert, consume, and transform all lower things, just as *Virgil* sings,
> *These herbs and these poisons gathered for me in Pontus*
> *Moeris himself gave to me; many grow in Pontus:*
> *By these I have often seen Moeris turn into a wolf and hide in the woods,*
> *And often seen spirits come forth from the depths of their graves,*
> *And seen sown crops transported to another place.* <note>original: Virgil, *Eclogues* 8.95-99. This passage highlights the classical belief that sorcery could manipulate both the natural world and the state of the soul.</note>

Likewise elsewhere, concerning the companions of *Ulysses*,
> *Whom the cruel goddess Circe, with her powerful herbs,*
> *Had changed from the likeness of men into the faces and bodies of wild beasts.* <note>original: Virgil, *Aeneid* 7.19-20.</note>

And a little later,
> *Picus, the tamer of horses, whom his wife Circe—captured by desire—*
> *Struck with her golden wand and, changed by her poisons,*
> *Made into a bird, and dappled his wings with colors:* <note>original: Virgil, *Aeneid* 7.189-191. Refers to the myth of King Picus being turned into a woodpecker.</note>

There are, moreover, types of such sorceries of the kind that *Lucan* recites concerning that *Thessalian* witch <note>Referring to Erictho, a notorious necromancer in Roman literature known for her gruesome rituals.</note> summoning the spirits of the dead, saying,
> *Here, whatever nature produced in an ill-omened birth*
> *Is mixed: the foam of dogs who fear water is not absent;* <note>original: "spuma canum, quibus unda timori est"; a reference to the saliva of rabid dogs.</note>
> *The entrails of a Lynx, and the knot of the tough Hyena*
> *Were not missing, nor the marrow of a Stag fed on a serpent;*
> *Nor the Remora* <term>Remora</term> <note>original: "Echeneis"; a legendary small fish believed to have the power to stop a ship in its tracks by clinging to the hull.</note> *which holds back the ship in the midst of the waters*
> *While the East Wind stretches the ropes; and the eyes of dragons.* <note>original: Lucan, *Pharsalia* 6.670-675.</note>

And such things are told by *Apuleius* concerning that sorceress *Pamphila* <note>A character from Apuleius’s *Metamorphoses* (The Golden Ass), known for her potent and dangerous magic.</note>, working a spell for love; to whom her maid *Fotis* brought goat hairs shorn from inflated wine-skins instead of the hairs of a *Boeotian* youth. *Fotis* brought them to *Pamphila*, saying:

> Now mad in her mind, she climbs the shingled roof, which—being open to the air on both sides of the house and stripped of cover to provide a view of all the eastern and other directions—she frequents in secret as a place most suited to these arts of hers. First, she prepared her deadly workshop with the usual equipment: every kind of spice, plates inscribed with mysterious letters <note>original: "laminis literatis"; these were likely metal amulets or lead "defixiones" (curse tablets) used to command spirits.</note>, and nails preserved from ill-fated ships; many limbs of the lamented and the buried were also set out. Then, having chanted over the breathing entrails, she makes an offering with various liquids: now with spring dew, now with cow’s milk, now with mountain honey, and she makes a libation of mead. Thus she takes those hairs, entwined and knotted together, and places them upon live coals to be burned with many perfumes. Then immediately, by the unassailable power of the Magical discipline and the blind violence of compelled spirits, those bodies whose singed hairs were smoking are changed into a human spirit; they feel, they hear, they walk, and they come to where the scent of their own remains leads them; and, yearning for entry instead of that *Boeotian* youth, they leap against the doors.

### On the Admirable Powers of Certain Sorceries <note>original: "ueneficiorum"; in this period, the term "veneficium" covered a range of meanings from herbal poisoning to harmful sorcery or "maleficium."</note> Chapter 42.

Now indeed, I shall relate certain sorceries, so that by their example, a path may be prepared for this entire investigation. Among these, therefore, is menstrual blood; let us see what great powers it possesses in sorcery. For as they say, by its arrival, new wines sour; the vine is permanently harmed by its touch; crops become sterile when touched; grafts die; the sprouts of gardens are scorched; and the fruits of trees fall off. The brilliance of mirrors is dimmed by its very gaze; the sharp edge of iron in razors and the luster of ivory are dulled; even iron is immediately corrupted by rust, and bronze, upon contact, acquires a heavy, foul-smelling poison and verdigris <note>original: "æruginem"; the green film formed on copper or bronze by oxidation.</note>. Dogs are driven into a frenzy if they taste it, and their bite is infected with an incurable poison. The hives of bees die off, and they flee if the hives are touched; linen turns black when boiled. Mares, if they are pregnant, suffer a miscarriage <note>original: "abortum"</note> upon contact with it, and it also causes a miscarriage if applied to pregnant women. She-asses do not conceive for as many years as the number of barley grains they have eaten that were touched by it; and the ash of menstrual cloths, if someone sprinkles it on clothes to be washed, changes the purple color, [and ...] flowers <note>The text likely continues to describe how the ash affects the color or health of flowers.</note>.

...takes away the color of [garments]. <note>This likely refers to purple-dyed fabrics, which ancient authors claimed were discolored by contact with menstrual blood.</note> They say that tertian and quartan fevers <note>These are recurring fevers typically associated with malaria; "tertian" fevers recur every third day, and "quartan" every fourth.</note> are driven away by menstrual blood [soaked] in the wool of a black ram and enclosed in a silver bracelet. Furthermore, for these same tertian and quartan fevers, it is said to be most effective to smear the soles of the patient's feet with it, and it is much more effective if done by the woman herself, even if she is unaware of the purpose; in this way, epileptic seizures <note>original: "comitiales impetus"; epilepsy was known as the "comitial disease" because a seizure was considered an ill omen that required the dismissal of the Roman *comitia*, or assembly.</note> and diseases are cured.

Furthermore, they say that if women who are menstruating walk through the fields while naked, caterpillars, small worms, beetles, blister beetles <note>original: "cantharides"; these beetles were used in medicine but were known to be toxic.</note>, and all harmful pests will fall off the crops. However, they must be careful not to do this at sunrise, for the seedlings will dry up. Similarly, it is said that hailstorms and whirlwinds are driven away by this, and it is useful against lightning; Pliny <note>Pliny the Elder (23–79 AD), the Roman naturalist whose "Natural History" is the source of many of these traditional folk beliefs.</note> himself recounts many of these things. 

You should know that it is a more potent poison if it occurs while the Moon is waning, but its power is greatest if it happens during the **silent Moon** <note>original: "silente Luna"; the phase of the New Moon when it is not visible in the sky.</note>. If, however, it happens during an eclipse of the Moon or Sun, it becomes incurable. It is of the greatest and most powerful vigor when that purging occurs in the first years [of maturity], and is during the initial virginity; it is also agreed that at such a time, if the doorposts of a house are touched with it, every **sorcery** <note>original: "maleficium"; specifically refers to harmful or "black" magic.</note> within that house is rendered powerless. 

Furthermore, they say that the threads of a garment touched by it cannot even be overcome by fire, and if they are thrown into a blaze, the fire will not spread any further. It is also said that if peony root <note>term: "Pæonia"; the peony plant was highly regarded in Renaissance medicine for its supposed power to treat nervous disorders and epilepsy.</note> is given to a patient along with **castoreum** <note>term: "Castoreum"; a yellowish secretion from the scent glands of a beaver, used in traditional medicine for its pungent odor to treat hysteria and other ailments.</note> and the scrapings from menstrual cloths, the **falling sickness** <note>Another common historical name for epilepsy.</note> is cured. Furthermore, if the stomach of a deer...

...if you have burned or roasted [the pests mentioned previously], and you add the smoke from burnt menstrual cloths, they say that <term>ballistae</term> <note>While usually referring to large military engines, in this context of hunting, it likely refers to spring-loaded traps or snares.</note> will be of no use for hunting. Also, the hairs of a menstruating woman, if placed under dung, are said to generate serpents; yet if they are burned, the odor drives serpents away. So great is the power of this <term>veneficium</term> <note>original: "ueneficij"; a Latin term that encompasses both poisoning and magical sorcery.</note> that it acts as a venom even against venomous creatures. 

There is also <term>Hippomanes</term> <note>Literally "horse-madness"; a legendary substance used in ancient Greco-Roman love magic.</note>, a small piece of flesh quite famous among magical potions, the size of a dried fig and black in color, which appears on the forehead of a newborn foal. Unless the mother devours it immediately, she loses all affection for her offspring and will not nurse it. Because of this, they say it has the greatest power to stir up love when, having been turned into a powder, it is drunk in cups mixed with the blood of a lover. 

There is another magical substance they call by the same name, Hippomanes: namely, that fluid which flows from the loins of mares at the time they are driven by the frenzy of desire. Virgil mentions this when he sings:
> Hence at last, the fluid trickles from the groin, which the 
> shepherds call by its true name, Hippomanes. 
> Hippomanes, which wicked stepmothers often gather, 
> mixing it with herbs and harmful spells.

<note>original: "Hippomanes carmenq; loquar, coctumq; uenenum / Priuigno datū." from Juvenal's *Satires*.</note>

...[having been] anointed, they become an object of hatred to all. They say the same happens with a boiled eye. Furthermore, the very end of the intestinal tract <note>The rectum or "extremam fistulam intestini."</note> is employed against the injustices of leaders and powers; it is said to be effective for the success of petitions, judgments, and the outcomes of lawsuits, if one carries even a tiny amount of it. Likewise, if the socket <note>original: "cauerna"; likely referring to an eye socket or a specific cavity from the animal previously discussed.</note> from the same animal is bound to the left arm, if the wearer looks at a woman, it is a love-spell so immediate that she follows him at once. However, the skin of the forehead resists enchantments <note>original: "fascinationibus"; the "evil eye" or malevolent spells cast through a gaze.</note>.

They also claim that the blood of a Basilisk <note>A legendary reptilian "king of serpents" said to kill with its glare.</note>, which they also call the "blood of Saturn," possesses such great power in <term>veneficium</term> <note>original: "ueneficio"; a term for magical craft, often involving potions, animal substances, or poisoning.</note> that it grants the one carrying it success in petitions to those in power, as well as answers to prayers from the gods, cures for diseases, and the granting of favors. They also say that a tick, if it is plucked from the left ear of a dog that is entirely black, possesses great power in divining whether someone will live. For if a sick person responds to the one who brought the tick—while that person stands at their feet and asks about the illness—there is certain hope for life. But the patient will die if they provide no answer.

They also say that a stone bitten by a rabid dog has the power to cause discord if it is placed in a drink. Furthermore, one will not be barked at by a dog if a dog’s tongue is placed in the shoe beneath the big toe, especially if it is paired with the herb of the same name, namely <term>cynoglossa</term> <note>literally "dog's tongue"; the herb *Cynoglossum officinale*, used in folk medicine and magic.</note>. The same is said to happen for one carrying a membrane from the afterbirth of a dog; moreover, dogs will flee from anyone carrying a dog's heart. Finally, Pliny <note>Gaius Plinius Secundus (Pliny the Elder), the Roman author of Natural History, who is a primary source for these magical traditions.</note> relates that there are frogs called <term>Rubetae</term> <note>Bramble-frogs or toads, believed in antiquity to be highly toxic and magically potent.</note>, which live only among briars and are full of magical powers, and that marvelous things are done with them. ### BOOK I.

...[to make] cold water immediately boil; the attacks of dogs are restrained by it; love is incited, as are quarrels when it is added to a drink; and if it is worn as an amulet <note>original: "adligatum"</note>, it stimulates lust. On the other hand, with the little bone found in the right side, boiling water is cooled and will not boil again unless the bone is removed; quartan fevers <note>A form of malaria where the fever returns every fourth day.</note> are healed with it when it is bound in a fresh snake-skin, as are other fevers; likewise, love and lust are inhibited by its presence. 

Moreover, the spleen <note>original: "lyen"</note> and the heart of these frogs provide an effective aid against the magical poisons <term>veneficia</term> <note>Agrippa uses this term to refer to both literal poisons and harmful magic or "sorcery" made from animal substances.</note> that are made from the frogs themselves; Pliny <note>Pliny the Elder, a Roman author whose "Natural History" is a primary source for this type of ancient lore.</note> recounts these things. It is also said that iron with which a person has been killed possesses a marvelous power among magical works; for if horse-bits <note>original: "morsus equi"</note> and spurs are made from it, they say that with these, any horse—even the most fierce—is most easily ruled and overcome. And if horse-shoes <note>literally "feet of the horse" or "shoes for the feet."</note> were made from such iron, they say the horse becomes exceptionally fast and never tires from any labor. 

They further claim that certain characters <note>Magical symbols or sigils.</note> and names should be inscribed upon these items. They also say that if someone dips a sword with which men are beheaded into wine, any sick person who drinks from this wine is freed from quartan fever. They also say that a potion made from a bear’s brain and served from the bear’s own skull induces a "bear-madness" <note>original: "ursinam rabiem"</note>, such that the man truly believes himself to be transformed into a bear. He judges everything he sees as having the appearance of bears and persists in that madness until the power of the potion is dissolved, though no other permanent harm to his physical constitution <note>original: "complexione"; referring to the balance of the four humors in the body.</note> is felt afterward.

### Concerning suffumigations, their rationale, and their power. Chapter 43.

Certain suffumigations <term>suffumigationes</term> <note>This refers to the ritual burning of substances to produce smoke or incense for magical or medicinal purposes.</note> adapted to the stars also possess great power, insofar as they strongly affect the air and the spirit <term>spiritus</term> <note>In Agrippa’s time, the "spirit" was seen as a subtle vapor within the body that connected the physical soul to the physical flesh.</note> to appropriately receive celestial gifts under the rays of the stars. For our own spirit is greatly transformed by vapors of this kind, since both are a type of vapor and are similar to one another. The air is also very easily affected by these vapors, taking on the qualities of both the lower and celestial realms; as it enters the breast <term>praecordia</term> <note>literally the area around the heart and diaphragm, then considered the seat of the emotions and vital breath.</note> continuously and repeatedly, it wonderfully reduces us to similar qualities. 

For this reason, those about to prophesy <note>original: "uaticinaturo"</note> are accustomed to using suffumigations to affect the imagination; these vapors, being in harmony with certain divine powers <term>numinibus</term>, prepare us to receive divine inspiration. Thus, it is said that incense made from flax seed, fleawort seed, the roots of violets, and parsley <note>original: "Apij"</note> allows one to see future things and aids in prophecy. 

Furthermore, let no one wonder how much power suffumigations have over the air, if they agree with Porphyry <note>A Neoplatonist philosopher who wrote extensively on the nature of spirits and the soul.</note> that certain vapors exhaling from their own specific suffumigations immediately attract and draw in spirits of the air <term>dæmones</term> <note>agrippa uses "dæmones" in the classical sense of "daemons"—spirits that inhabit the air—rather than purely "demons" in the modern sense of evil beings.</note>. These can induce thunder, lightning, and similar phenomena. For example, it is well known that the liver of a chameleon burned on the highest roof tiles incites rain and lightning. Similarly, the head and throat, if set ablaze with oak wood, are said to cause a gathering of rain and thunder. Suffumigations are also performed under favorable stellar influences to make the images <note>original: "idola"</note> of spirits appear immediately in the air or elsewhere; thus they say, if made from coriander, and parsley, or henbane <note>original: "Hyoscya"</note>...

<meta>catchword: mo</meta>

<summary>This page discusses how the vapors from ritual incense (suffumigations) can alter the human spirit and the surrounding air to facilitate prophecy and summon spirits. It explains the "sympathetic" link between the physical smoke, the stars, and the spiritual realm.</summary>

<keywords>suffumigation, incense, prophecy, Porphyry, spirits, chameleon, elemental magic, astrology, imagination</keywords>

->BOOK I.<-

...greatest degree <note>The text begins with the suffix "-mo," completing the word "maximo" (greatest) from the previous page.</note>, for when a fumigation is made with Hemlock <note>original: "Cicuta"</note>, demons immediately assemble; for this reason, they call those the "herbs of the spirits." Similarly, they say that if a preparation is made from the root of the Cane-reed <note>original: "Cannæ ferulæ"</note> with the juice of Hemlock, and Henbane <note>original: "Hyoscyami"</note>, and Mullein <note>original: "Tassi Barbassi"</note>, and red Sandalwood, and black Poppy, and a fumigation <term>suffitus</term> is performed, it causes demons and strange figures to appear. But if Parsley <note>original: "Apium"</note> is added to these, it puts demons to flight from every place and destroys their idols <note>Referring here to the spectral "images" or "idols" projected by the spirits.</note>. 

In a similar way, a fumigation made from Calamint, Peony, Mint, and Palma Christi <note>literally "the Palm of Christ," a common name for the Castor Oil plant, *Ricinus communis*</note> drives away all evil spirits and harmful phantoms. Furthermore, they relate that by certain suffumigations, certain animals are gathered or put to flight; just as Pliny reports that by burning the Liparis stone <note>A legendary stone mentioned by Pliny the Elder in his *Natural History* as being able to summon all kinds of animals.</note>, all beasts are summoned. Likewise, by burning the bones from the upper throat of a Stag, serpents are gathered, but by burning a stag’s horn, they are put to flight. 

Burning the feathers of Peacocks has the same effect. Similarly, when the lung of an ass is set on fire, all poisonous things flee. If the hoof of a horse is burned in any house, mice are driven away; the same happens with the hoof of a mule. Flies are also driven away by it, if the hoof is from the left foot. 

They also say that if a house or some place is fumigated with the gall of a Cuttlefish <note>original: "felle Sepiæ"</note> mixed with incense, roses, and aloes-wood, and if some sea water or blood is then cast into that place, the whole house will seem full of water or blood. If plowed earth is cast there, the earth will seem to tremble. Moreover, one must not think otherwise than that vapors of this kind create a certain "body" and infuse power into the air, and persist for a very long time

They say, for instance, that if someone buries gold, or silver, or anything precious, while the Moon is joined to the Sun at the bottom of the sky <note>original: "imo cœli"; referring to the *Imum Coeli* or the Fourth House in astrology, associated with foundations and hidden things</note>, and fumigates the place with coriander, saffron, Henbane <note>original: "Hyoscyamo"</note>, Parsley, and black poppy—beaten together in equal weights and mixed with the juice of Hemlock—that which is so hidden can never be found or taken away, and demons will always guard it. Furthermore, if anyone strives to take anything from there, he will be infested by them and fall into a frenzy <note>original: "phrenesim"; a state of delirium or madness</note>. 

And Hermes <note>Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary Hellenistic figure to whom many foundational texts of alchemy and magic are attributed.</note> says that ambergris <note>original: "sperma ceti"; though literally "whale sperm," in historical perfumery and magic this refers to the fatty substance from the sperm whale used as a fixative.</note> in a fumigation has no equal for attracting demons. Therefore, if a fumigation is made from it along with wood of aloes, costus <note>An aromatic root used in ancient incense</note>, musk, saffron, and incense, mixed with the blood of a hoopoe <note>The hoopoe bird was frequently associated with magical operations in the Solomonic and Hermetic traditions.</note>, it very quickly gathers aerial spirits. And if a fumigation is made with it around the tombs of the dead, it gathers the shades <note>original: "manes"</note> and shadows of the dead. 

Thus, whenever we direct some work toward the Sun, we fumigate with solar substances; if toward the Moon, with lunar ones; and they did similarly concerning the rest. And it should be known that just as there is contrariety and enmity among the stars and spirits, so also in fumigations toward...

Spirits brought in by the smoke of aloeswood will flee when sulfur is lit. Proculus <note>A 5th-century Neoplatonist philosopher who wrote on the "hieratic arts" or divine magic.</note> gives an example of this: a spirit that was accustomed to appearing in the form of a lion immediately vanished when a rooster was presented, because the rooster is naturally contrary to the lion. This principle of opposition must be carefully considered and practiced in similar matters.

### The Composition of Certain Fumigations Adapted to the Planets Chapter 44.

Thus for the **Sun**, we create a fumigation <term>suffumigatio; the ritual burning of incense to attract or repel specific spiritual forces</term> from saffron, ambergris <note>original: "ambra"; in this context, the fragrant substance from sperm whales, not the fossilized resin</note>, musk, aloeswood, balsam wood, and the fruit of the laurel tree, along with cloves, myrrh, and frankincense. All of these are crushed and mixed in a specific proportion—namely, that which yields the sweetest scent. They are then incorporated with the brain of an eagle or the blood of a white rooster into the form of pills or lozenges <note>original: "trochiscorum"</note>.

Indeed, we make the **Lunar** fumigation from the dried head of a frog, the eyes of a bull, and white poppy seed, along with frankincense and camphor. These are incorporated with menstrual blood or the blood of a goose.

For **Saturn**, we take the seed of the black poppy, the seed of Henbane <note>original: "Hyoscyami"; a toxic plant in the nightshade family used for its sedative and hallucinogenic properties</note>, mandrake root, loadstone <note>original: "lapis magnes"; a naturally magnetized piece of the mineral magnetite</note>, and myrrh. These are prepared with the brain of a cat or the blood of a bat.

For **Jupiter**, one takes the seed of the ash tree, aloeswood...

There are, furthermore, the incenses of **Saturn**: all fragrant roots, such as costus <note>an aromatic root from India used in ancient perfumes</note> and the frankincense-herb <note>original: "herbæ thuris"; often referring to rosemary or a similar aromatic plant</note>. The incenses of **Jupiter** are all fragrant fruits, such as nutmeg and cloves. Those of **Mars** are every fragrant wood: sandalwood, cypress, balsam, and aloeswood. Those of the **Sun** are every gum: frankincense, mastic, benzoin, storax, ladanum <note>a sticky resin obtained from the Cistus (rockrose) shrub</note>, as well as ambergris and musk. Those of **Venus** are flowers: roses, violets, saffron, and the like. Those of **Mercury** are all barks of woods and fruits, such as cinnamon, cassia-bark, mace, citron peels, and laurel berries, and also any fragrant seeds. The **Moon** has for its incense the leaves of all plants, such as Indian leaf <term>folium Indum; Malabathrum, a spice and perfume ingredient from the Himalayas</term>, and the leaves of myrtle and laurel.

Furthermore, they can induce a transposition of those powers which are within the spirit itself, so that it may now act not only upon its own body, but also upon what is nearby, and through [visual] rays...

This is apparent in dreams, where sights present themselves to us more often than sounds or other sensations. Therefore, when eye-salves transform the visual spirits, that spirit easily affects the imagination; and when the imagination is affected by various appearances and forms, it transmits them through the same spirit to the external sense of sight. They also say that if a steel mirror is anointed with the juice of mugwort <note>original: "artemisiae"</note> and fumigated, it causes one to see summoned spirits within it.

In the same way, certain fumigations <note>the use of magical incense or smoke</note> or ointments are made that cause those who sleep to speak, walk, and perform the deeds of those who are awake—and even sometimes things which those awake could scarcely do or dare. This is the same reason why those who are manic or melancholic <note>in the medical theory of the time, these were conditions caused by an imbalance of the "humors," or bodily fluids</note> believe they see or hear from the outside things which the imagination only creates <term>phantasticatur; the process where the "phantastic" or imaginative faculty of the soul generates mental images</term> from within. Hence, they fear things that are not to be feared, fall into wondrous and entirely false suspicions, and flee when no one is pursuing them. They grow angry and struggle when no one else is present, and fear where there is no fear.

Passions of this kind can also be induced by magical concoctions: through fumigations, eye-salves, ointments, potions, sorceries <note>original: "veneficia"; refers to the use of magical drugs, potions, or poisons</note>, through lamps and lights, through mirrors, through images, through incantations and songs, and through sounds and harmonies composed of the sinews <note>tendons or nerves used as strings</note> of certain animals according to a specific harmony; they can even be caused by various rites, observations,

He writes that this actually happened to the father of a man named Praestantius. Even the Holy Scriptures testify that Pharaoh’s magicians changed their rods into dragons, and water into blood, and other such things.

Therefore, through these bindings <note>original: "alligationes"</note>, suspensions, wrappings, placements, and contacts, the accidents <term>accidents; in philosophy, these are non-essential qualities or states of being, such as being happy or sick, rather than the essence of the thing itself</term> of the body and soul are changed: into sickness or into health, into boldness or into fear, into sadness or into joy, and the like. They render those who wear them graceful or terrible, accepted or rejected, honored and beloved, or hated and abominable. 

It is believed that passions of this kind are infused through the aforementioned means no differently than is manifest in the grafting of trees <note>original: "insitionibus arborum"</note>, where the vital virtue is transferred from the trunk into the graft <note>original: "surculum"</note> bound to it, even though the contact points do not naturally grow together. Thus, in the female palm tree, when she is near the male, her branches lean toward him and are bent; seeing this, gardeners tie ropes from the male to the female, and she then returns to standing upright, as if she had already obtained the male's virtue through the continuity of the rope. 

In a similar way, we see that the sea torpedo <note>a type of electric ray known since antiquity for its ability to deliver a shock</note>, even when touched from a distance through a very long rod, suddenly numbs the hand of the person touching it. Furthermore, if anyone should touch a sea hare <note>original: "leporem marinum"; a type of large sea slug, which ancient authors like Pliny believed was toxic to the touch</note>, either with a hand or a stick while it is in a diseased state, they suffer a fainting fit <note>original: "animis defectum"</note>. Similarly, they say that the starfish...

It is necessary, however, to know the specific law and method of binding and suspending that is suitable for this art: namely, that these acts be done under a specific and harmonious constellation <note>the relative positions of stars and planets at a specific time</note>. Let the aforementioned bindings and suspensions be made with metallic threads, or silk, or from hair, or nerves, or fur, or the bristles of certain animals. The wrappings, indeed, should be made in the leaves of herbs, or the skins of animals, or certain small cloths and similar materials, so that the things agree among themselves. 

For instance, if you were seeking to procure the solar virtue <note>original: "solarem... uirtutem"; the spiritual or magical power associated with the Sun</note> of some object for yourself, you should hang it from your neck wrapped in a laurel leaf, or the skin of a lion, with a golden thread or silk of a saffron color, while the Sun is ruling in the figure of heaven <note>original: "figura cœli"; an astrological chart showing the positions of the planets</note>; for in this way, you will be able to become the master <note>original: "compos"</note> of the solar virtue of that thing. But if you desire the virtue of some Saturnine thing <note>original: "saturnalis rei"; something under the astrological influence of the planet Saturn</note>, you should wrap it in a similar way while Saturn is dominating, using the skin of a donkey or a funeral cloth—especially if you are working toward sadness—and hang it with a black thread. One should judge the remaining planets in a similar manner.
